\left|z^{\prime}_{t,b}-M\cdot\mathcal{P}_{t}(\circ,b)\right|\leqslant 1,

(43)

for every
b\in B
. We let
\mathcal{R}_{t}
be the distribution over
B
given by

\mathcal{R}_{t}(b)=\frac{z^{\prime}_{t,b}}{M}.

Note that
\mathcal{R}_{t}
is the discretized version of the marginal of
\mathcal{P}_{t}
over
B
given by
Lemma

7.3
.

Apply
Lemma

8.1
to
\mathcal{D}
with the nonnegative integers
C_{t,b}
therein set to
C_{t,b}=z^{\prime}_{t,b}
to get a dPCP of the form

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\left(\left(\cup_{t\in[n]}S_{t}\right)\cup A,E_{2},\Sigma^{d}_{A}\times\Sigma_{0},\Sigma_{A},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E_{2}},\{D_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right),

where
S_{t}=\bigcup_{b\in B}\{(t,b)\}\times[z^{\prime}_{t,b}]
for each
t\in[n]
. Note that the sets
S_{t}
are disjoint and that when defining
S_{t}
, if
z^{\prime}_{t,b}=0
we think of
\{(t,b)\}\times[z^{\prime}_{t,b}]
as the empty set. Furthermore, for each
t
,

|S_{t}|=\sum_{b\in B}z^{\prime}_{t,b}=M.

(44)

By
Lemma

8.1
,
\mathcal{D}_{2}
has perfect completeness,
(L\cdot{\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),2\varepsilon^{1/5})
-list-decoding soundness, and decoding degree
1
. Likewise, we get that if
\mathcal{D}
is right-regular, then
\mathcal{D}_{2}
is left-regular.

Next, we will modify the decoding distributions of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
to obtain our final dPCP,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
. Our goal is to obtain a dPCP whose complete decoding distribution has uniform marginal over the left vertices (and we will later show that this implies the decoding distribution guarantee of the theorem statement). Let
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be the complete decoding distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. By the decoding distribution guarantee of
Lemma

8.1
, we have that

\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\circ)=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{P}_{t}(\circ,b)}{z^{\prime}_{t,b}}=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{p_{t,b}}{z^{\prime}_{t,b}}

The above shows that the marginal of
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
over the left vertices is almost uniform, and hence we will only need to make a small modification.

We note that for each
t,b
and
i
, the distribution of
a\sim\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\cdot)
is equal to
\mathcal{Q}(t,\cdot,b)
. Indeed, for any
a\in A
, using the decoding distribution guarantee of
Lemma

8.1
we have

\frac{Q_{2}(t,(t,b,i),a)}{\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\circ)}=\frac{\mathcal{Q}(t,a,b)/C_{t,b}}{\mathcal{Q}(t,\circ,b)/C_{t,b}}=\frac{Q(t,a,b)}{Q(t,\circ,b)},

where in the second transition we used the fact that (using the decoding distribution guarantee of
Lemma

8.1
again)

\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\circ)=\sum_{a\in A}\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),a)=\sum_{a\in A}\frac{\mathcal{Q}(t,a,b)}{C_{t,b}}=\frac{\mathcal{Q}(t,\circ,b)}{C_{t,b}}.

On the other hand, consider the distribution
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
, generated by choosing
t\in[n]
uniformly,
b\sim\mathcal{R}_{t}
,
a\sim\mathcal{Q}(t,\cdot,b)
,
i\in[z^{\prime}_{t,b}]
uniformly and outputting
(t,(t,b,i),a)
. Then for any
t
and
(t,b,i)
, we have

\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\circ)=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{z_{t,b}^{\prime}}{M}\cdot\frac{1}{z_{t,b}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{M}.

Now we show that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
is close to
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
in TV-distance. First note that for every
t,b
and
i
, by definition the distributions
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,\cdot,b)=\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\cdot)
, which in turn implies that,
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\cdot)
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\cdot)
are identical. Therefore, the TV-distance between
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
is equal to
the TV-distance between their marginal on
(t,b,i)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathsf{TV}(\mathcal{Q}_{2},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2})\leqslant\sum_{t\in[n],b\in B,i\in[z^{\prime}_{t,b}]}\left|\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\circ)-\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,(t,b,i),\circ)\right|\leqslant\sum_{t\in[n],b\in B,i\in[z^{\prime}_{t,b}]}\left|\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{p_{t,b}}{z^{\prime}_{t,b}}-\frac{1}{M}\right)\right|.

Since the summation is only taken over
t,b
such that
z^{\prime}_{t,b}\geqslant 1
, so we can bound each summand there as

\frac{1}{n}\left|\frac{p_{t,b}}{z^{\prime}_{t,b}}-\frac{1}{M}\right|=\frac{1}{n}\left|\frac{Mp_{t,b}-z^{\prime}_{t,b}}{Mz^{\prime}_{t,b}}\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{nMz_{t,b}^{\prime}},

where we used (
43
). Hence, putting everything together, we get

\mathsf{TV}(\mathcal{Q}_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{2}^{\prime})\leqslant\frac{|B|}{M}.

(45)

Our final dPCP, which we call
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, is obtained by taking
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and changing its complete decoding distribution to
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
. In particular all of the parts of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are the same as in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
except for the decoding distributions. We have,

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\left(\left(\cup_{t\in[n]}S_{t}\right)\cup A,E_{2},\Sigma^{d}_{A}\times\Sigma_{0},\Sigma_{A},\{\Phi^{\prime}_{e}\}_{e\in E_{2}},\{D_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right),

where for each
t\in[n]
, the new decoding distribution
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2,t}
is the distribution over
E_{2}
given by
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
.

It is clear that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
still has perfect completeness and the degree related parameters, projection decision complexity, and decoding complexity are the same as in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. By (
45
),
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
has
(L\cdot{\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),2\varepsilon^{1/5}+|B|/M)
-list decoding soundness.
Finally, for each
t\in[n]
, it can be checked that the decoding distribution,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2,t}
, chooses a vertex in
S_{t}
uniformly at random, and furthermore
|S_{t}|=M
. Hence, we can view the left vertices as
[n]\times[M]
and the decoding distribution
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{2,t}
is indeed as described in the lemma.
∎

We now describe how to obtain the inner dPCP that we will use for composition.

Theorem 8.3

(Inner dPCP)
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
there exist
L={\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon)
and functions
M,K:\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
satisfying

M(T,N)=O_{T}(N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))\quad\text{and}\quad K(T,N)=O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))

such that the following holds. For every alphabet
\Sigma_{0}
of size at most
T
and circuit
\varphi:\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
of size at most
N
, the language
{\sf SAT}(\varphi)
has a projection dPCP of the form

\mathcal{D}=\left(\left([n]\times[M]\right)\cup B,E,\Sigma_{A},\Sigma_{B},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E},\{D_{t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right)

which satisfies the following with
M:=M(T,N)
and
K:=K(T,N)

•

Length.

O_{T}(nM+N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Alphabet Sizes.
The left and right alphabet sizes are
2^{\left(O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))\right)}
and
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
respectively.

•

Degrees.

\mathcal{D}
has decoding degree
1
.

•

Projection Decision Complexity.

O_{|T|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Decoding Complexity.

O_{|T|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Decoding Distribution.
Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the complete decoding distribution. Then,
\mathcal{Q}
satisfies the following:

–

For each
(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]
and
b\in B
we can write
\mathcal{Q}(\circ,(t,r),b)=\frac{w((t,r),b)}{nMK}
for some
w((t,r),b)\in\mathbb{N}
.

–

For each
t\in[n]
, the distribution
\mathcal{Q}(t,\cdot,\circ)
is uniform over
\{t\}\times[M]
and hence for every
(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]
,

\sum_{b\in B}w((t,r),b)=K

•

Completeness.
The dPCP has perfect completeness.

•

List-Decoding Soundness.
The dPCP has
(L,\varepsilon)
-list-decoding soundness.

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
, fix sizes
T
and
N
and let
\varphi:\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
be a circuit of size at most
N
with
|\Sigma_{0}|\leqslant T
. We take
\mathcal{D}_{{\sf dp}}
to be the dPCP for
{\sf SAT}(\varphi)
in
Theorem

6.1
with soundness parameter set to
\varepsilon
:

\mathcal{D}_{{\sf dp}}=(X(k),X(\sqrt{k}),E_{{\sf dp}},\Sigma^{k},\Sigma^{\sqrt{k}},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E_{{\sf dp}}},\{D_{{\sf dp},t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{{\sf dp},t}\}_{t\in[n]}).

Now set
M=O_{T}(N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
sufficiently large relative to
|X\left(\sqrt{k}\right)|=O_{T}(N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
. We also have that there is some
d_{r}={\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)
such that regardless of the initial circuit
\varphi
, the right degree of every vertex is at most
d_{r}
. We will transform
\mathcal{D}_{{\sf dp}}
into the desired inner dPCP.

Decoding Degree Reduction + Right Alphabet reduction.

We first apply
Lemma

8.2
with
M
set as above, and then apply right alphabet reduction from
Lemma

7.1
with sufficiently small
\eta={\sf poly}(\varepsilon)
on the resulting dPCP. Call the dPCP after both transformations
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and let its constraint graph be
([n]\times[M]\cup B,E)
. Note that we can assume such structure on the left vertices by
Lemma

8.2
. Call the resulting complete decoding distribution
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
. Let us list a few properties we have that are straightforward to verify from
Lemma

8.2
and
Lemma

7.1
.

•

Length.

|B|=|X(k)|\cdot O_{\eta}(\sqrt{k}\log|\Sigma|)=O_{T}(N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.
This follows from the length guarantees of
Lemma

8.2
and the structure of the resulting constraint graph from
Lemma

7.1
. The length is therefore
nM+|B|=O_{T}(nM+N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Alphabets.
The left alphabet has size at most
|\Sigma|^{k\cdot d_{r}}\cdot T=2^{O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))}
after applying
Lemma

8.2
, and this is unaffected by
Lemma

7.1
. The right alphabet has size at most
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
by
Lemma

7.1
.

•

Degrees.
The decoding degree is
1
after applying
Lemma

8.2
and this is unaffected by
Lemma

7.1
. The degree of each left vertex is at most
O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
. This follows from the left degree guarantee of
Lemma

8.2
(which turns right degree of
\mathcal{D}_{{\sf dp}}
into left degree) and the fact that
Lemma

7.1
multiplies the left degree by
O_{\eta}(\sqrt{k}\log|\Sigma|)=O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
. We refer to
Theorem

6.1
to bound
\sqrt{k}\log|\Sigma|\leqslant O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Projection Decision Complexity.

O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
. This is straightforward to check by using
Theorem

6.1
, then
Lemma

8.2
, and finally
Lemma

7.1
.

•

Decoding Complexity.

O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
. This is straightforward to check by using
Theorem

6.1
, then
Lemma

8.2
, and finally
Lemma

7.1
.

•

Decoding Distribution.
The marginal of
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
over
[n]\times[M]
is uniform and each left vertex
(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]
has exactly one decoding neighbor,
t
. This follows from the decoding distribution and decoding degree guarantees of
Lemma

8.2
,

•

Completeness.

\mathcal{D}_{2}
has perfect completeness because
\mathcal{D}_{{\sf dp}}
does and both
Lemma

8.2
and
Lemma

7.1
preserve it.

•

Soundness.

\mathcal{D}_{2}
has
(L\cdot{\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),O(\varepsilon^{1/5}))
soundness by
Theorem

6.1
,
Lemma

8.2
, and
Lemma

7.1
. Here we use the fact that
M
is sufficiently large relative to
|X(\sqrt{k})|
.

At this point, we have all of the desired properties, except for the discreteness of the marginal of the complete decoding distribution over the edges (i.e., the first guarantee of the decoding distribution above). We resolve this next, and some care is needed to accomplish this while also preserving a uniform marginal over the left vertices.

Discretizing the Complete Decoding Distribution in a Left Uniform Manner.

We construct a discretized version of the distribution
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\cdot)
for each
(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]
. Specifically, note that
|\supp(\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\cdot))|
is upper bounded by the degree of
(t,r)
, which is
O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
. We apply
Lemma

7.3
with denominator set to a sufficiently large
M^{\prime}=O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
and let
\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}}
be the distribution obtained. Here we again note that we can choose the same
M^{\prime}
regardless of the specific circuit
\varphi
fixed at the start. By
Lemma

7.3
and the fact that
M^{\prime}
is sufficiently large, we have

\mathsf{TV}(\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}},\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\cdot))\leqslant\varepsilon

(46)

and
\supp(\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}})\subseteq\supp(\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\cdot))
. Now consider the following complete decoding distribution
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
:

•

Choose
(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]
uniformly.

•

Choose
b\sim\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}}
.

•

Output
(t,(t,r),b)
,

and let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
be the dPCP obtained by changing the complete decoding distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
from
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
to
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
.

Since
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
have the same marginal distribution over
[n]\times([n]\times[M])
, we have

\begin{split}\mathsf{TV}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{2})&=\sum_{(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]}\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\circ)\cdot\mathsf{TV}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,(t,r),\cdot),\mathcal{Q}(t,(t,r),\cdot))\\
&=\sum_{(t,r)\in[n]\times[M]}\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\circ)\cdot\mathsf{TV}(\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}},\mathcal{Q}(t,(t,r),\cdot))\\
&\leqslant\varepsilon,\end{split}

(47)

where the last transition uses (
46
). We now verify that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
satisfies all of the desired properties. The length, degree, projection decision complexity, and decoding complexity are all unchanged from
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and hence follow by the items above. Perfect completeness is preserved in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
because
\supp(\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}})\subseteq\supp(\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,(t,r),\cdot))
as noted above, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
still has
(L\cdot{\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),O(\varepsilon^{1/5}))
-soundness because of (
47
). This shows the desired soundness condition because
\varepsilon
can be taken arbitrarily small and we can reparameterize the list size. Finally, we check the two properties of the decoding distribution. First, it is clear from the above that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
satisfies that for each
t\in[n]
, the distribution
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,\cdot,\circ)
over
\{t\}\times[M]
is uniform, so this takes care of the second condition there. For the first condition, we note that for any
(t,r)\in[n]\times[M],b\in B
we can write

\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}(t,(t,r),b)=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{M}\cdot\Delta_{t,r,\mathsf{disc}}(b)=\frac{w((t,r),b)}{nMM^{\prime}},

for some
w((t,r),b)\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, the first condition follows for
K(T,N)=M^{\prime}=O_{T}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.
∎

8.2
An Outer dPCP

The following result will be used as our outer dPCP.
Compared to
Lemma

8.2
, the construction below has quasi-linear length and a complete decoding distribution whose marginal over the right vertices is uniform.

Theorem 8.4
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
there exist
d^{\prime},L={\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon)
such that the following holds. For any alphabet
\Sigma_{0}
and circuit
\varphi:\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
of size at most
N
, the language
{\sf SAT}(\varphi)
has a projection dPCP of the form

\mathcal{D}=(A\cup B,E,\Sigma_{B}^{{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)}\times\Sigma_{0},\Sigma_{B},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E},\{D_{t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{t}\}_{t\in[n]})

which satisfies the following

•

Length.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}(N\cdot{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Alphabet Sizes.
The left and right alphabet sizes are
2^{O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))}
and
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
respectively.

•

Degrees.
The dPCP has decoding degree
1
.

•

Projection Decision Complexity.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Decoding Complexity.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}\left({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)\right)
.

•

Decoding Distribution.
The complete decoding distribution,
\mathcal{Q}
, satisfies

–

The marginal of the complete decoding distribution over
A\times B
is
K
-discrete for some

K=O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}(N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)).

–

Writing
\mathcal{Q}(\circ,a,b)=\frac{w(a,b)}{K}
for integers
w(a,b)
, we have
\sum_{a\in A}w(a,b)=d^{\prime}
for all
b\in B
and hence the marginal over the right vertices is uniform.

–

For each left vertex
a
,
\sum_{b\in B}w(a,b)\leqslant O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Completeness.
The dPCP has perfect completeness.

•

List-Decoding Soundness.
The dPCP has
(L,\varepsilon)
-list-decoding soundness.

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
, and let
\mathcal{D}_{1}=(X(k),X(\sqrt{k}),E_{1},\Sigma^{k},\Sigma^{\sqrt{k}},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E_{1}},\{D_{1,t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{1,t}\}_{t\in[n]})
be the dPCP from
Theorem

6.1
with
(L,\varepsilon)
-list-decoding soundness where
L={\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon)
. This dPCP has decoding degree, left degree, and right degree that are
{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N),O_{\varepsilon}(1),{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)
respectively, and its complete decoding distribution, which we call
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
, is agnostic and has marginal over
X(k)\times X(\sqrt{k})
which is
K
-discrete for some
K=N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)
. By the decoding distribution guarantee of
Theorem

6.1
, for each
\textbf{b}\in X(\sqrt{k})
, we have

\mathcal{Q}_{1}(\circ,\circ,\textbf{b})\leqslant\frac{{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)}{N}.

(48)

Starting from
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, we will first apply decoding degree reduction (
Lemma

8.1
) where all of the
C_{t,b}
therein are set to
1
, and then apply right alphabet reduction (
Lemma

7.1
). We call the resulting dPCP
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and obtain our final dPCP, which we call
\mathcal{D}_{3}
, by applying right degree reduction on
\mathcal{D}_{2}
.

Decoding Degree Reduction + Right Alphabet Reduction.

First, we note that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
has agnostic complete decoding distribution by
Theorem

6.1
, so we may apply
Lemma

8.1
with the
C_{t,b}
therein all set to
1
. We then apply
Lemma

7.1
with
\eta=O(\varepsilon)
. The resulting dPCP,

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\left(A_{2}\cup B_{2},E_{2},\Sigma_{A},\Sigma_{B},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E_{2}},\{D_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right),

has the following properties, which are straightforward to verify.

•

Completeness.
Perfect completeness.

•

Length.
We have
|A_{2}|\leqslant|X(\sqrt{k})|\cdot{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)
and
|B_{2}|\leqslant O_{\varepsilon}(|X(k)|\cdot\log(|\Sigma|^{k}))
, so the overall length is
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}(N\cdot{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Degrees.
The left degree is
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
, the decoding degree is
1
, and the right degree is
{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)
.

•

Projection Decision Complexity.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

Decoding Complexity.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

•

List-Decoding Soundness.

({\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),O(\varepsilon^{1/5}))
-list-decoding soundness.

In order to apply right degree reduction next, we need to analyze the complete decoding distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, which we call
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
. Fix an arbitrary
a_{2}\in A_{2}
and let us bound
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\circ,a_{2},\circ)
. First, since the decoding degree is
1
, there is some
t
such that

\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\circ,a_{2},\circ)=\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t,a_{2},\circ)=\mathcal{P}_{2,t}(a_{2},\circ)

Then, by the decoding distribution guarantee of
Lemma

8.1
, there is some
\textbf{b}\in X(\sqrt{k})
, such that

\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\circ,a_{2},\circ)=\mathcal{P}_{2,t}(a_{2},\circ)=\mathcal{P}_{1,t}(\circ,\textbf{b})=\mathcal{Q}_{1}(t,\circ,\textbf{b}).

Now applying (
48
), we have

\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\circ,a_{2},\circ)=Q_{1}(t,\circ,\textbf{b})\leqslant Q_{1}(\circ,\circ,\textbf{b})\leqslant\frac{{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)}{N}.

(49)

Right Degree reduction.

Having analyzed the complete decoding distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, we are now ready to apply
Lemma

7.4
on it. Note that we need to apply the version of right degree reduction in
Lemma

7.4
, which changes the complete decoding distribution, because we did not analyze the discreteness of the marginal of
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
over the edges.

To this end, set

M=\Theta_{|\Sigma_{0}|}\left(\frac{N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)}{n}\right)

sufficiently large and degree parameter
d^{\prime}
sufficiently large relative to
\varepsilon
and apply
Lemma

7.4
with these parameters.
To see that we have set
M
large enough to apply
Lemma

7.4
, we use the fact that
\mathcal{D}_{2}
has left degree at most
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
, and
|A_{2}|\leqslant N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)
.
Call the resulting dPCP

\mathcal{D}_{3}=\left(A_{2}\cup B_{3},E_{3},\Sigma_{A},\Sigma_{B},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E_{3}},\{D_{2,t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{3,t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right),

and let
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
be the complete decoding distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{3}
.
By
Lemma

7.4
there are positive integers
w(a,b)
for each
(a,b)\in A_{2}\times B_{3}
such that we can write
\mathcal{Q}_{3}(\circ,a,b)=\frac{w(a,b)}{d^{\prime 2}nM}
.
Fix a left vertex
a
of
\mathcal{D}_{3}
. By the decoding distribution guarantee of
Lemma

7.4
, we have that either,

\mathcal{Q}_{3}(\circ,a,\circ)\leqslant\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\circ,a,\circ)+\frac{|\Gamma_{2}(a)|}{nM}\leqslant\frac{O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))}{N}

(50)

or

\mathcal{Q}_{3}(\circ,a,\circ)\leqslant\frac{1}{nM}+\frac{\varepsilon}{10|A_{2}|}\leqslant\frac{1}{N}.

(51)

In the above inequalities,
\Gamma_{2}(a)
, denotes
a
’s neighborhood in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and the first inequality uses
Lemma

7.4
while the second inequality uses the fact that
\mathcal{D}_{2}
has left degree
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
and
|A_{2}|\geqslant N
. We can now verify the listed properties for
\mathcal{D}_{3}

Length.

The right side has size
d^{\prime}nM=O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(N\log N))
and the left side has size
|A_{2}|
, so the final length is
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}(N{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
.

Alphabet Sizes.

The left alphabet size is
|\Sigma_{A}|=2^{O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))}
and the right alphabet size is
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
because we applied
Lemma

7.1
.

Degrees.

The decoding degree is
1
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and this is preserved by
Lemma

7.4
when going to
\mathcal{D}_{3}
.

Projection Decision Complexity.

The decision complexity is
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and this is preserved by
Lemma

7.4
.

Decoding Complexity.

The decoding complexity is
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and this is preserved by
Lemma

7.4
.

Decoding Distribution.

The complete decoding distribution is
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
which is
K
-discrete for

K:=d^{\prime 2}nM=O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}(N\cdot{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)).

The second item in the decoding distribution condition, follows because the complete decoding distribution guarantee of
Lemma

7.4
results in
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
having uniform marginal over the right vertices. The third item follows from (
50
) and (
51
) as for any
a
we have

\sum_{b\in B}w(a,b)=K\cdot\mathcal{Q}_{3}(\circ,a,\circ)\leqslant O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)).

Completeness.

We have perfect completeness in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and this is preserved by
Lemma

7.4
.

Soundness.

It is straightforward to check that the
\mathcal{D}_{3}
has
({\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),O(\varepsilon^{1/5}))
-list-decoding soundness by the list-decoding soundness of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and
Lemma

7.4
.
∎

9
A Composition Theorem for Decodable PCPs

This section proves a composition theorem for dPCPs, which facilitates left alphabet reduction. In
Section

10
we show how to apply it on the constructions from
Section

8
(with a final composition step with a standard Hadamard-code based dPCP) to reduce the alphabet size from
2^{{\sf poly}(\log(N))}
all the way down to
O(1)
.

Our composition result is as follows. Throughout the proof, we will use bold letters (e.g.,
\textbf{a},\textbf{b}
) for outer dPCP vertices, and non-boldface symbols for inner dPCP vertices (e.g.
a,b
).

Theorem 9.1
.

Suppose we have the following two dPCPs, which we call outer dPCP and inner dPCP.

•

Outer dPCP.
There exists
C_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
\varepsilon>0
there exist
d_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
and functions
F_{1},\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1},

\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{1},K_{1},k_{1}:\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
such that every circuit
\varphi:\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\Sigma
of size
N
over some alphabet
\Sigma_{0}
has a dPCP as follows

–

Parameters.
Length
S_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)
, left and right alphabets of sizes
F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)
and
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
respectively, decoding degree
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
, projection decision complexity at most
\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)
, and decoding complexity at most
\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)
.

–

Complete Decoding Distribution.
Call the complete decoding distribution
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}
and the sides of the constraint graph
A_{\mathsf{out}},B_{\mathsf{out}}
. Then, the following holds

*

(Discreteness): for every
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}},\textbf{b}\in B_{\mathsf{out}}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\textbf{b})=\frac{\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})}{K_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)}
for
\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\in\mathbb{N}
.

*

(Right Uniform): for every right vertex
b
,
\sum_{\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}}\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})=d_{1}
, and hence the marginal of
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}
is uniform over
B_{\mathsf{out}}
.

*

(Left Bounded): for every left vertex
a
,
\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a}):=\sum_{\textbf{b}\in B_{\mathsf{out}}}w(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\leqslant k_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)
.

–

Completeness.
Perfect completeness.

–

Soundness.

(\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon)
-list-decoding soundness where
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}=\varepsilon^{-C_{1}}
.

•

Inner dPCP.
There exists
C_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
\varepsilon>0
there are bivariate functions
F_{2},\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{2},

\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2},K_{2}:\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
and
M:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
such that every circuit
\varphi:\left(\Sigma^{\prime}_{0}\right)^{n^{\prime}}\to\Sigma
of size at most
N
over some alphabet
\Sigma^{\prime}_{0}
of size at most
T^{\prime}
has a dPCP as follows.

–

Parameters.
Length
S_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
, left and right alphabets of sizes
F_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
and
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
respectively, decoding degree
1
, projection decision complexity at most
\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
, and decoding complexity at most
\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.

–

Complete Decoding Distribution.
Call the complete decoding distribution
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in}}
and the sides of the constraint graph
A_{{\sf in}},B_{{\sf in}}
. Then the following holds:

*

(Discreteness): for every
a\in A_{{\sf in}},b\in B_{{\sf in}}
, we have
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in}}(\circ,a,b)=\frac{\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}(a,b)}{n^{\prime}\cdot M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})\cdot K_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})}
for
\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}(a,b)\in\mathbb{N}
.

*

(Left Uniform): the left vertices can be labeled as
[n^{\prime}]\times[M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})]
, so that for every
t\in[n^{\prime}]
, the distribution
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in}}(t,\cdot,\circ)

is uniform over

\{t\}\times[M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})]
. It follows that the marginal distribution of
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in}}
over the left vertices is uniform and for every left vertex
a
,
\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}(a):=\sum_{b\in B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}(a,b)=K_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.

–

Completeness.
perfect completeness.

–

Soundness.
(\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon)
-list-decoding soundness where
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}=\varepsilon^{-C_{2}}
.

Then for every
\varepsilon>0
, and every circuit
\varphi:\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
of size
N
, the language
{\sf SAT}(\varphi)
has a projection dPCP with the following properties, where we set

N^{\prime}=O(k_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)+\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))\quad\qquad\text{and}\quad\qquad T^{\prime}=O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(1).

•

Length.

S_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)\cdot\left(M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})+S_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})\right)
.

•

Left alphabet size.

F_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})^{d_{1}}
.

•

Right alphabet size.

O_{\varepsilon}(1)
.

•

Decoding Degree.

O_{\varepsilon}(d_{1})
.

•

Projection Decision Complexity.

O(\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)))+O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})+\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})).

•

Decoding Complexity.

\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))+\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.

•

Complete Decoding Distribution.
Call the complete decoding distribution
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}
and the sides of the constraint graph
A_{\mathsf{comp}},B_{\mathsf{comp}}
. Then the following holds:

–

(Discreteness):
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}(\circ,a,b)=\frac{\mathsf{wt}^{\prime\prime}(a,b)}{J}
for
\mathsf{wt}^{\prime\prime}(a,b)\in\mathbb{N}
and
J=K_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)\cdot M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})\cdot K_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.

–

(Left Uniform): The marginal of
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}
over
A_{\mathsf{comp}}
is uniform, and for every
a\in A_{{\sf comp}}
we have
\sum_{b\in B}\mathsf{wt}^{\prime\prime}(a,b)=d_{1}K_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.

•

Completeness.
The dPCP has perfect completeness.

•

Soundness.
The dPCP has
({\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),\varepsilon)
-list-decoding soundness.

Proof overview.

Our proof strategy follows the lines of the proof of
[
52
]
and
[
28
]
. Specifically, we construct a version of the inner dPCP for each left vertex
a
in the outer dPCP. The size of the circuit relevant for a given left vertex is determined by (1) the projection decision complexity, (2) the parameter
k_{1}
which should be thought of as a weighted version of left degree (with weights coming from the complete decoding distribution), and (3) the decoding degree. In our application all of these are
O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}(\log(N)))
or
O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(1)
, so the inner dPCP is run on a much smaller scale.

In order to compose all of these smaller inner dPCPs into one overall dPCP, we attempt to check several constraints “in parallel”. Namely, as in
[
52
,
28
]
we think of choosing a right vertex
b
of the outer PCP and checking all of the inner PCPs of
a
that are neighbours of
b
. This requires some alignment
between the inner PCPs of various
a
, which is handled by the extra flexibility we have arranged in
Section

8
in the size of the PCPs.
Additional care is needed to make sure that the decoding is still maintained throughout, and this aspect is where we differ from the prior compositions mentioned. Indeed, this is why we went through extensive efforts in
Lemma

8.1
to reduce the decoding degree of the dPCP from
Theorem

6.1
to
1
. Roughly speaking, having small decoding degree allows us to pass decoding to the inner dPCPs while not increasing their alphabet size by too much.

9.1
The Construction

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and a size
N
circuit
\varphi:\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
. Our goal is to construct a dPCP for the language
\mathcal{L}:={\sf SAT}(\varphi)\subseteq\Sigma_{0}^{n}
, and we have the outer dPCP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
and the inner dPCP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in}}
at our disposal. We will apply the outer dPCP to
\varphi
and the inner dPCP for circuits
\varphi_{\textbf{a}}
of size roughly
O(k_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)+O(\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))
, which should be thought of as much smaller than
N
.

The Outer dPCP.

Take
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
to be the outer dPCP for
\mathcal{L}
as in the premise with the soundness error set to
\delta_{\mathsf{out}}:=\varepsilon^{C_{2}+10}
. We denote its parts by

\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}=\left(A_{\mathsf{out}}\cup B_{\mathsf{out}},E_{\mathsf{out}},\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{out}}},\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}},\{\Phi_{\mathsf{out},e}\}_{e\in E_{\mathsf{out}}},\{D^{\mathsf{out}}_{t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}^{{\sf out}}_{t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right).

Let
q_{1}
denote the decoding degree of
\mathcal{D}_{{\sf out}}
so that
q_{1}=O_{\varepsilon}(1)
by assumption.

Henceforth, it will be convenient to let
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
denote the set of
a
’s neighbors listed with multiplicity
\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})
. In order to avoid confusion when referring to multiple copies of
b
(which appears in
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
more than once if
\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})>1
), we refer to the members of
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
as
(\textbf{b},i)
where for each
\textbf{b}\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
,
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
. Hence,

\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})=\bigcup_{b\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})}\{\textbf{b}\}\times[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})].

Notice that choosing
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
uniformly, the marginal distribution of
b
is
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\cdot)
.

The following notation will be useful.

Definition 9.2
.

For a symbol
\sigma
for
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
and
\textbf{b}\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
, let
\sigma_{\to\textbf{b}}
denote the unique
\Sigma_{B}
symbol that satisfies the projection constraint on
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})
. For each
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf dec},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
, we write
\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow t}=D^{\mathsf{out}}_{t}{}(\textbf{a},\sigma)
. Here, the vertex
a
will always be clear from context.

The Inner dPCPs.

We will also construct an inner dPCP corresponding to each outer vertex
a
. For each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
we start by defining a language

\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}\subseteq\left(\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}\times\Sigma_{0}^{q_{1}}\right)^{\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})},

which is supposed to correspond to the set of valid alphabet symbols for
a
. Set,

T^{\prime}=|\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}|\times|\Sigma_{0}|^{q_{1}}=O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(1),

(52)

to be the size of the alphabet which each of the languages
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
is over. In the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
, we identify
[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})]
with the multiset
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
. Let us now fix an
a
and define the language
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
.

For each
w\in\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
, think of its
\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})
indices as being indexed by
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
and for each
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
we write
w_{(\textbf{b},i)}
as the symbol in
w
at index
(\textbf{b},i)
. The symbol
w_{(\textbf{b},i)}
is thought of as consisting of one
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
symbol corresponding to an implied label for
b
, and
q_{1}
symbols from
\Sigma_{0}
, corresponding to one implied label for each
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
. With this setup in mind, the members of
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
are exactly the
w\in\left(\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}\times\Sigma_{0}^{q_{1}}\right)^{\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})}
such that there exists
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
satisfying the following two conditions:

•

for every
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
, the
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
symbol in
w_{(\textbf{b},i)}
, which we denote by
\left(w_{(\textbf{b},i)}\right)_{\textbf{b}}
, is equal to
\sigma_{\to\textbf{b}}
,

•

for every
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
and
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
, the symbol corresponding to
t
in
w_{(\textbf{b},i)}
, which we denote by
\left(w_{(\textbf{b},i)}\right)_{t}
, is equal to
\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow t}
.

In words, the above two conditions mean that all of the symbols in
w
are consistent with a single
\sigma\in\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}
.

By the projection decision complexity of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
and the bound on
\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})
, the language
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
can be decided by a circuit of size
O(\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})+\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1}(N))=O(k_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)+\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))
, and hence there exists a uniform bound

N^{\prime}=O(k_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)+\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))

(53)

such that for
every

\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
, membership in the language
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
can be decided by a circuit of size
at most

N^{\prime}
. Henceforth, we fix

M=M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad K_{2}=K_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime}),

(54)

where
M(\cdot,\cdot)
is the function related to the left vertices of the inner dPCP given by the theorem statement, and
K_{2}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the function related to the complete decoding distribution of the inner dPCP given by the theorem statement.

Now, for each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
we take
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
to be the inner dPCP guaranteed by the theorem statement for the language
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
with soundness error set to

\delta_{\mathsf{in}}:=\varepsilon.

Note that we can use the same value
M
and
K_{2}
here for every inner dPCP,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. This is because the inner dPCP assumption in the theorem statement, along with the fact that for all
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
, the language
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
is over strings of length at most
k_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)
with an alphabet of size at most
T^{\prime}
, and membership in
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
can be decided by a circuit of size at most
N^{\prime}
.

For each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
, we denote the parts of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
as

\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}=(A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\cup B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}},E_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}},\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}},\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}},\{\Phi_{\mathsf{in},e}\}_{e\in E_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}},\{D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}\}_{(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})},\{\mathcal{P}^{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}\}_{(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})}).

We have that the alphabet sizes are

|\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}|\leqslant F_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad|\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}|=O_{\varepsilon}(1).

(55)

For, convenience, we will express
A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
as
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})\times[M]
, rather than
[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})]\times[M]
, where recall that
|\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})|=\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})
. Also, for simplicity, we refer to members of
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})\times[M]
as
(\textbf{b},i,r)
(rather than
((\textbf{b},i),r)
.

We will also use the following simplifying notation, which is similar to that in
Definition

9.2
, but now specified for the inner dPCP.

Definition 9.3
.

Given a symbol
\sigma\in\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
for
(\textbf{b},i,r)\in A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, recall that
D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{b},i,r),\sigma)
outputs a
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
symbol for
b
and one
\Sigma_{0}
symbol for each
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
. We let
\left(\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow(\textbf{b},i)}\right)_{\textbf{b}}
denote the
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
symbol part of the output from
D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{b},i,r),\sigma)
. Likewise, for each
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf dec},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
, we write
\left(\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow(\textbf{b},i)}\right)_{t}
to denote the
\Sigma_{0}
symbol for
t
in
D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{b},i,r),\sigma)
. When we use this notation, the identity of
r\in[M]
will be clear from context.

By design of the language, the output of the decoder,
D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}
consists of one
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
symbol for
b
and one
\Sigma_{0}
symbol for each
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
. The list-decoding soundness of
D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}
translates to the following claim, which morally states that one can view
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
as a decodable PCP for the language of valid
a
-alphabet symbols, with
(\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}},\delta_{{\sf in}})
-list decoding soundness.

Claim 9.4
.

For every
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
the following holds. For any left assignment
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}:\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})\times[M]\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
to
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, there is a list
\{\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{1},\ldots,\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}\subseteq\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
such that for any right assignment
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}:B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\to\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}

\Pr_{\textbf{b}\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\cdot),i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})],r\in[M],b\sim\mathcal{P}^{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{b},i,r),\cdot)}[\mathcal{E}_{1}\land\mathcal{E}_{2}]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{in}}.

Here
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
are the following events over the sampled
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a}),r\in[M],b\in B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
.

•

\mathcal{E}_{1}.
the constraint on
(\textbf{b},i,r)
and
b
is satisfied by
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{b},i,r)]
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[b]
.

•

\mathcal{E}_{2}.
the symbols for
(\textbf{b},i)

and
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
output by the decoder are not (simultaneously) equal to
\left(\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\to\textbf{b}}
and
\left(\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}
for any
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
. That is, for each
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
, either

\left(D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{b},i,r),T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{b},i,r)])\right)_{\textbf{b}}\neq\left(\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\to\textbf{b}}

or there is
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
such that

\left(D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{b},i,r),T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{b},i,r)])\right)_{t}\neq\left(\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}.

Proof.

Fix
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
. Applying the list-decoding soundness of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, we get a list of words
\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
such that for any right assignment
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
, the list-decoding error of
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
relative to this list is at most
\delta_{\mathsf{in}}
. We can translate this list,
\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}
, into a corresponding list of
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}
symbols
\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{1},\ldots,\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\in\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
. Specifically, for each
w_{\ell}\in\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}
, by definition of being in
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
, we have that there exists
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
such that for any
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
and
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})

(w_{\textbf{b},i})_{\textbf{b}}=\sigma_{\to\textbf{b}}\quad\text{and}\quad(w_{\textbf{b},i})_{t}=\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow t}.

We set
\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{\ell}
to be this symbol and if for some
w\in\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}
there exist multiple such
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
, we choose one of them arbitrarily to be
\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{\ell}
. Now it is straightforward to see that the list-decoding soundness of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
for the language
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
relative to the list
\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}
translates exactly into the statement of the claim with the list
\{\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{1},\ldots,\sigma^{\textbf{a}}_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}\subseteq\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
. Here, we use the fact that sampling
(\textbf{b},i)
in the multiset,
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
, uniformly, we have
\textbf{b}\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\cdot)
and
i
uniform in
[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
.
∎

Finally, let us write the complete decoding distribution of each inner dPCP,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, as
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}
. Using the discreteness property of the inner dPCP’s complete decoding distribution, we have that for every
((\textbf{b},i,r),b)\in A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\times B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, there is
\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i,r),b)
such that

\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}(\circ,(\textbf{b},i,r),b)=\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i),(\textbf{b},i,r),b)=\frac{\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i,r),b)}{\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})\cdot M\cdot K_{2}}.

(56)

By the third property of the complete decoding distribution of the inner dPCP, for all
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
and
(\textbf{b},i,r)\in A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
we have

\sum_{b\in\Gamma_{\textbf{a},{\sf in}}((\textbf{b},i,r))}\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i,r),b)=K_{2},

(57)

where in the above
\Gamma_{\textbf{a},{\sf in}}
denotes the neighborhood of a vertex in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. We emphasize that, by assumption,
K_{2}
above is the same for all of the inner dPCPs,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. We are ready to describe our composed dPCP.

The composed dPCP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}\circ\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in}}
.

•

Constraint Graph:
The constraint graph is bipartite with the following left and right sides respectively:

\displaystyle A_{\mathsf{comp}}\coloneqq\{(\textbf{b},r)\ :\ r\in[M]\text{ and }\ \textbf{b}\in B_{\mathsf{out}}\},

\displaystyle B_{\mathsf{comp}}\coloneqq\{(\textbf{a};b)\ :\ \textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}},b\in B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\}=\bigcup_{\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}}\{\textbf{a}\}\times B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}.

Let us explain how the vertices in the composed PCP should be thought of. First, take the inner constraint graphs,
(A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\cup B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}},E_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}})
, for each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
. Viewing each left side as
\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})\times[M]
, we see that for each
\textbf{b}\in B_{\mathsf{out}}
and
r\in[M]
, there are
d_{1}
-many vertices of the form
(\textbf{b},i,r)
which appear in
A_{\mathsf{comp}}
. This is because of the assumption that

\sum_{\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}}\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})=d_{1},

(58)

from the outer dPCP’s complete decoding distribution. Henceforth, when we are in the context of the composed dPCP, we will denote the copy of
(\textbf{b},i,r)
in
A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
by
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
.

The left side of the composed constraint graph is obtained by identifying, for each
\textbf{b}\in B_{\mathsf{out}}
and
r\in[M]
, the vertices of
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
over all
a
adjacent to
b
and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
. One can think of the vertex
(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
as a cloud consisting of all
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
appearing over all copies of the inner dPCPs
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
.

The right side of the composed constraint graph is the union of the right sides of the inner constraint graph, and the edges are the same after identification.

•

Left Alphabet:
Fix a vertex
(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
. For each
\textbf{a}\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{b})
and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
, there is a vertex
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
coming from the inner dPCP of
\mathcal{D}_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}
. Then, the composed vertex
(\textbf{b},r)
holds a symbol from
\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}},\textbf{a}}
for each
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
. Overall, using (
58
) see that these are
d_{1}
inner left alphabet symbols.

If
\sigma
is an alphabet symbol for
(\textbf{b},r)
, we use
\sigma\{\textbf{a},i\}
to denote the
\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
symbol corresponding to a label for
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
. We also constrain the alphabet of
(\textbf{b},r)
to only contain
\sigma
as follows.

–

Hardcoding Outer Constraints.

We require that
\left(\sigma\{\textbf{a},i\}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}
is the same for all
\textbf{a}\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc}}(\textbf{b})
and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
. Henceforth, we denote this value as
\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}
so that

\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}=\left(D^{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),\sigma\{\textbf{a},i\})\right)_{\textbf{b}}.

(59)

On the right side, we are first running the inner decoder from
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, where the index to decode is
(\textbf{b},i)
, the left vertex is
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)\in A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, and the symbol is
\sigma\{\textbf{a},i\}
. We then take the symbol for
b
given by the output symbol. We note that whenever we use the notation of (
59
), the symbol
\sigma
will satisfy the hardcoding outer constraints, so all the possible
a
lead to the same value.

–

Hardcoding Decoding Consistency.
For any
t\in[n]
and all
\textbf{a},i
such that
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\in\supp(\mathcal{P}_{t})
and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
, we require that the decoded values
\left(D^{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}_{\textbf{b}}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),\sigma\{\textbf{a},i\})\right)_{t}
are the same, and we denote this value by
\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow t}
.

We denote the left alphabet by
\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}
.

•

Right Alphabet:
The right alphabet is
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
.

•

Constraints:
By our construction,
((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\in E_{\mathsf{comp}}
if and only if there is an
i
such that
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
and, in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, the vertex
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
is adjacent to
(\textbf{a};b)
. Let us describe the constraint on this edge now.

Given valid labels
\sigma\in\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
for
(\textbf{b},r)
and
(\textbf{a};b)
respectively in the composed dPCP, the constraint on
((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))
checks that, for all such
\textbf{a},i
as above, where
(\textbf{b},i,r)
is adjacent to
b
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, the inner constraint of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, between
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
and
(\textbf{a};b)
is satisfied by the labels
\sigma\{\textbf{a},i\}\in\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
.
That is,

\Phi_{((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=1

if and only if

\Phi_{{\sf in},((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b))}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=1

over all
\textbf{a},i
such that
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
is adjacent to
(\textbf{a};b)
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. In the second equality, the constraint is coming from
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
.

•

Decoding Distributions:
Let
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}
be the complete decoding distribution of the outer dPCP. Then the complete decoding distribution of the composed dPCP,
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}
, is generated as follows.

–

Choose
(t,\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}
, and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
uniformly at random.

–

Sample
r\in[M]
uniformly.

–

Choose
(\textbf{a};b)\sim\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i),(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),\cdot)
,

–

Output
(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))
.

It is clear that the marginal of
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}
over
[n]
is uniform, and for each
t\in[n]
we take the decoding distribution to be
\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{comp}}_{t}=\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
.

•

Decoder.
For

(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
and label
\sigma
, the decoder outputs
D^{\mathsf{comp}}_{t}((\textbf{b},r),\sigma)=\sigma_{\rightsquigarrow t}
.

This completes the description of the composed dPCP,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}\circ\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in}}
, and we will now show that it satisfies the requirements of
Theorem

9.1
.

9.2
Basic Properties of the Composed PCP

In this section we establish all of the properties of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}\circ\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in}}
as promised in the statement of
Theorem

9.1
, except for the list-decoding soundness, which is deferred to the next section. In the below, recall that
T^{\prime}
is given by (
52
),
N^{\prime}
is given by (
53
),
K_{2}
and
M
are given by (
54
).

Length.

By construction, left side has size at most
|B_{\mathsf{out}}|\cdot M
vertices and the right side has size
\sum_{\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}}|B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}|
, so overall the length is

S_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)\cdot M(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})+S_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)\cdot S_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime}).

Alphabet Sizes.

The left alphabet size is at most
|\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}|^{d_{1}}
.
Indeed, fix a left vertex
(\textbf{b},r)
. Then for every
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
,
(\textbf{b},r)
holds one symbol from
\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
, corresponding to a label to
(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
,
which by (
58
) is a total of
d_{1}

symbols from
\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
. Hence the alphabet size is
F_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})^{d_{1}}
, where we use (
55
) to bound the size of
|\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}|
. The right alphabet is
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
and its size is
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
by (
55
).

Decoding Degree.

The decoding degree is
O_{\varepsilon}(d_{1})
. Each
(\textbf{b},r)
is responsible for decoding
t
if there is
\textbf{a}\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{b})
such that
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
. The decoding degree bound follows from the fact that
|\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})|\leqslant O_{\varepsilon}(1)
and that

|\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{b})|\leqslant d_{1}
by (
58
).

Projection Constraints.

It is straightforward to check that the composed dPCP has projection constraints because the inner dPCPs are all projection dPCPs.

Projection Decision Complexity.

Fix a constraint
((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))
and let
\sigma
be the left alphabet symbol. Recall that
\sigma
contains, as its restrictions, one
\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
symbol for each of
d_{1}
vertices corresponding to all
\textbf{a},i
such that
\textbf{a}\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc}}(\textbf{b}),i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
.

The projection circuit needs to do the following:
(1) check that the left alphabet symbol satisfies hardcoding of outer constraints, (2) check that the left alphabet symbol satisfies hardcoding decoding consistency, and (3) compute the unique right alphabet symbol that satisfies the constraint.

Each of (1) and (2) can be done by computing a restriction of the given left alphabet symbol and then running the inner decoder
d_{1}
times and then checking an equality between
d_{1}
symbols of either
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
, in the case of (1), or
\Sigma_{0}
in the case of (2). Altogether, the circuit complexity of (1) and (2) is a constant multiple of

\displaystyle\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))+d_{1}\cdot(\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})+\log|\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}|+\log|\Sigma_{0}|)

\displaystyle=\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))+O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})).

To accomplish (3), the projection circuit runs the projection circuit of the inner dPCP,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, on a restriction of the given left alphabet symbol which requires circuit complexity

\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))+\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime}).

Overall this gives projection decision complexity that is,

O(\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)))+O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(\mathsf{DecisionComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})+\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})).

Decoding Complexity.

The decoder restricts its input to obtain a symbol for the inner dPCP and runs the decoder of the inner dPCP. Hence, the decoding complexity is
\log(F_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|))+\mathsf{DecodeComp}_{2}(N^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.

Complete Decoding Distribution.

Fix
(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
and
(\textbf{a};b)\in B_{\mathsf{comp}}
. Then

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}(\circ,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))&=\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\cdot\frac{1}{M}\cdot\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]}\left[\frac{\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i),(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b))}{\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}((\textbf{b},i),(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),\circ)}\right]\\
&=\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\cdot\frac{1}{M}\cdot\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]}\left[\frac{\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b))}{K_{2}}\right]\\
&=\frac{\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})}{K_{1}MK_{2}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]}\left[\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b))\right]\\
&=\frac{\sum_{i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]}\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b))}{K_{1}MK_{2}}\end{split}

(60)

In the first transition, we are using the definition of
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}
. In the second transition, we are using (
56
) to plug in the term inside the expectation, and then (
57
). In the third transition, we are writing out
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\textbf{a},\textbf{b})
. In the final transition, we are expanding out the expectation from the second line.

By the above, we get that the first property of the complete decoding distribution holds with discreteness parameter

J=K_{1}\cdot M\cdot K_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{wt}^{\prime\prime}((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))=\sum_{i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]}\mathsf{wt}^{\prime}_{\textbf{a}}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b)).

(61)

We move onto the second property which requires showing that the marginal over
A_{{\sf comp}}
is uniform. From the description, it is clear that

\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}(\circ,(\textbf{b},r),\circ)=\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\circ,\textbf{b})\cdot\frac{1}{M}=\frac{d_{1}}{K_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)M}.

In the second equality, we use the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\circ,\textbf{b})=\frac{d_{1}}{K_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)}
, by the right uniformity property of the outer dPCP’s complete decoding distribution. It follows that for any
(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}

\sum_{(\textbf{a};b)\in B_{\mathsf{comp}}}\mathsf{wt}^{\prime\prime}((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))=\frac{Jd_{1}}{K_{1}(N,|\Sigma_{0}|)M}=d_{1}K_{2},

as desired.

Completeness.

Since the outer dPCP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
has perfect completeness, for any
w\in\mathsf{SAT}(\varphi)
there are assignments
T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}:A_{\mathsf{out}}\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}:B_{\mathsf{out}}\to B_{\mathsf{out}}
such that all of the constraints in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
are satisfied and the decoder outputs
w_{t}
with probability
1
for all
t\in[n]
. Now, for each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
, using the value
T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}[\textbf{a}]
, one can construct
w_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\in\left(\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}\times\Sigma_{0}^{q_{1}}\right)^{\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a})}
such that for every
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
and
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
, we have that the
\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
part of
(w_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}})_{(\textbf{b},i)}
equals
(T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}[\textbf{a}])_{\to\textbf{b}}
, and the symbols corresponding to
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
in
(w_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}})_{\textbf{b}}
are consistent
\left(T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}[\textbf{a}]\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}
. For each such
w_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, which is in
\mathcal{L}_{\textbf{a}}
, one can then use the perfect completeness of the inner dPCP’s to construct
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}:A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}:B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\to B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
which satisfy all of the constraints on the inner dPCP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
and always output a decoding that is consistent with
(w_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}})_{\to\textbf{b}}
for all
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
and
(w_{{\sf in},\textbf{a}})_{\rightsquigarrow t}
for all
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
.

From these assignments, we construct the composed assignments
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}:A_{\mathsf{comp}}\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}:B_{\mathsf{comp}}\to\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}
as follows:

•

For every
(\textbf{a};b)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
, note that
b
is a right vertex in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, and hence we set
T_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]=T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[b]
.

•

For each
(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
,
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]
consists of the label
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{b},i,r)]
for each
\textbf{a}\in\Gamma_{{\sf out}}(\textbf{b})
and
i\in[\mathsf{wt}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})]
.

One can check that this assignment indeed satisfies all of the constraints of the composed dPCP and the decoder outputs
w_{t}
with probability
1
for all
t\in[n]
.

9.3
Proof of List-Decoding Soundness

This section is devoted to the proof of the following lemma, establishing the list-decoding soundness of
Theorem

9.1
and thereby completing its proof.

Lemma 9.5
.

The composed dPCP constructed above has decoding error
\delta_{{\sf in}}+\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}\cdot\delta_{{\sf out}}=\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{10}
relative to a list of size
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}\cdot\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}=\varepsilon^{C_{1}+C_{2}+10}
, and hence satisfies
({\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon),O(\varepsilon))
-list-decoding soundness.

Proof.

Fix an assignment.
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}:A_{\mathsf{comp}}\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}

We will first define a list of assignments for
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
. Specifically, we will have a left outer assignment,
T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}:A_{\mathsf{out}}\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}
, for each
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
.

Obtaining assignments to the left side of the Outer PCP.

For each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
, we will attempt to obtain candidate assignments to
a
in the outer dPCP by using the list-decoded values of the inner dPCP,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. To do so, we first use
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}
to obtain an assignment
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
for
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, and subsequently apply the list-decoding condition of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
to these assignments. Set

T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)]:={T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]}\{\textbf{a},i\},\quad\forall(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)\in A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}.

Now that
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
is an assignment to
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
, we can apply the list-decoding guarantee from
9.4
. We get that there is a list
\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}=\{y^{\textbf{a}}_{1},\ldots,y^{\textbf{a}}_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\}\subseteq\Sigma_{\textbf{a}}
such that the following holds for any right assignment
T^{\prime}:B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}\to\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
,

\Pr_{(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a}),r\in[M],(\textbf{a};b)\sim\mathcal{P}^{{\sf in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),\cdot)}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{in},1}\land\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{in},2}\right]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{in}}.

(62)

Here,
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{in},1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{in},2}
are the following events:

•

\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{in},1}
(the inner constraint is satisfied): this is the event that
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)]
and
T^{\prime}[(\textbf{a};b)]
satisfy the constraint on
((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a},b))
from
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
.

•

\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{in},2}
(the inner decoding fails): this is the event that the inner decoder does not output something consistent with the list. That is, the symbols for
b
and
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf out},{\sf dec}}(\textbf{a})
output by the decoder are not (simultaneously) equal to
\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\to\textbf{b}}
and
\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}
for any
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
.

Using
\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
we can now define
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}
assignments to
A_{\mathsf{out}}
. More specifically, for each
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
, define the assignment
T^{(j)}_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}:A_{\mathsf{out}}\to\Sigma_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}
by

T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}[\textbf{a}]={y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}},\quad\forall\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}.

Outer List decodings for each
T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}
.

Fix a
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
and consider one of the left outer assignments
T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}
. By the list-decoding condition of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{out}}
, there exists a list
\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{out}}^{(j)}=\{w^{(j)}_{1},\ldots,w^{(j)}_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{L}
such that for any assignment to the right side of the outer dPCP,
T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}:B_{\mathsf{out}}\to\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
, the list-decoding error relative to
\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{out}}^{(j)}
is bounded by
\delta_{\mathsf{out}}
. Specifically, we have that for each
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
and any right assignment
T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
,

\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in[n]\\
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\sim\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{out}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{out},1}\land\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{out},2}\right]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{out}},

(63)

where the events above are as follows:

•

\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{out},1}
(satisfied outer constraint): this is the event that
\Phi_{\mathsf{out},(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})}\left(T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}[\textbf{a}],T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}[\textbf{b}]\right)=1
, meaning that the outer constraint is satisfied on
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})
.

•

\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{out},2}
(the outer decoding fails): this is the event that
D^{\mathsf{out}}_{t}(\textbf{a},T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}[\textbf{a}])\notin\left\{{\left(w^{(j)}_{\ell}\right)}_{t}\right\}_{\ell\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}]}
, meaning that the decoding does not agree with any member of
\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{out}}^{(j)}
.

Showing List-Decoding Soundness.

We are now ready to show that the composed dPCP for
\mathcal{L}
satisfies
(\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}\cdot\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}},\delta_{\mathsf{in}}+\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}\cdot\delta_{\mathsf{out}})
-list-decoding soundness. The list-decoding soundness will be with respect to the following list from
\mathcal{L}
:

\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{comp}}:=\bigcup_{j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]}\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{out}}^{(j)}=\{w^{(j)}_{\ell}\}_{j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}],\ell\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}]}.

Fix any right assignment
T_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}:B_{\mathsf{comp}}\to\Sigma_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
. For each
\textbf{a}\in A_{\mathsf{out}}
define the following inner right assignment:

T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]:={T_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]},\quad\ \forall(\textbf{a};b)\in B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}.

Let us also define a candidate right assignments for the outer dPCP. For each
(\textbf{b},r)\in A_{\mathsf{comp}}
, recall that
{T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]}_{\textbf{b}}
gives an assignment to
\textbf{b}\in B_{\mathsf{out}}
. For each
r\in[M]
, recalling the notation from (
59
), we define a table
T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(r)}
as

T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(r)}[\textbf{b}]={T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]}_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}.

Now, we define the following events:

•

\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},1}
: this is the event that the constraint on
(\textbf{b},r)
and
(\textbf{a};b)
is satisfied in the composed dPCP:

\Phi_{\mathsf{comp},((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))}\left(T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)],T_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]\right)=1.

Unpacking the definition of the constraint, this is also the event that the constraint on the edge
((\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))
of the
inner
dPCP is satisfied by the induced assignments,
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)]
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]
.

•

\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},2}
(the composed decoding is outside the list): is the event that

D^{\mathsf{comp}}_{t}((\textbf{b},r),T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)])\notin\left\{{\left(w^{(j^{\prime})}_{\ell}\right)}_{t}\right\}_{{j^{\prime}\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}],\ell\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}]}}

meaning the output of the decoder for the decoding set
t
is
not
consistent with
w^{j}_{\ell}
for any
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
or
\ell\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}]
.

In the notation of the events above, showing list-decoding soundness of the composed dPCP amounts to showing the following inequality, where the term on the left hand side is precisely the list-decoding error.

\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},1}\land\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},2}\right]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{in}}+\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}\cdot\delta_{\mathsf{out}}.

(64)

It will be convenient now to define a few events. Call the event on the left hand side of (
64
)
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},1}\land\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},2}
and define the following events over
t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b)
output by the distribution from (
64
):

•

\mathcal{E}_{1}
is the event that there does not exist
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
such that
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}=\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\to\textbf{b}}
and
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]_{\rightsquigarrow t}=\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}
for all
t
such that
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\in\supp(\mathcal{P}^{{\sf out}}_{t})
.

•

\mathcal{E}_{2}
is the complementary event of the above. That is, that there does exist
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
such that
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}=\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\to\textbf{b}}
and
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]_{\rightsquigarrow t}=\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}
for all
t
such that
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\in\supp(\mathcal{P}^{{\sf out}}_{t})
.

It is clear that
\mathcal{E}=\left(\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}\right)\lor\left(\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{2}\right)
so the probability of interest is

\begin{split}\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}]&=\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}]+\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}\;\land\;\mathcal{E}_{2}]\\
&\leqslant\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}]+\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}_{{\sf comp},2}\;\land\;\mathcal{E}_{2}].\end{split}

(65)

We start by bounding
\Pr[\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}]
. We think of
(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}
as being sampled in the following manner. First,
\textbf{a}\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\cdot,\circ)
is sampled, then
(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a}),r\in[M]
are chosen uniformly at random. Finally,
t
is chosen according to the marginal
\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\cdot,\textbf{a},\textbf{b})
, and
(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))
is output. Rewriting the distribution in this manner, one can check that we have

\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}]\leqslant\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\textbf{a}\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf out}}(\circ,\cdot,\circ)}\left[\Pr_{(\textbf{b},i)\in\Gamma_{\mathsf{disc},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a}),r\in[M]}[\mathcal{F}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b},i,r}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}]\right],

(66)

where
\mathcal{F}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b},i,r}
is the event that the assignments,
T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)]:=T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]\{\textbf{a},i\}
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]:=T_{B_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{a};b)]
, satisfy the constraint on
((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),(\textbf{a};b))
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. We note that the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
contains the event that the inner decoding on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
for
(\textbf{b},i)
is not consistent with any member of
\mathsf{List}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
. That is, that for every
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
, either,

\left(D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)])\right)_{\textbf{b}}=T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]_{\rightsquigarrow\textbf{b}}\neq\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\to\textbf{b}}

or for some
t\in\Gamma_{{\sf dec},{\sf out}}(\textbf{a})
,

\left(D^{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}_{(\textbf{b},i)}((\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r),T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}[(\textbf{a};\textbf{b},i,r)])\right)_{t}=T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]_{\rightsquigarrow t}\neq\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}.

In the above two equations we are using the hardcoding of the alphabet of
(\textbf{b},r)
. Specifically, the equality in the first equation is by the “hardcoding outer constraints” and the equality in the second equation is by the “hardcoding decoding consistency”.

Hence for each
a
, the inner probability on the right hand side of (
66
) is at most the list-decoding error of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}
applied to left and right assignments
T_{B_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}},T_{A_{\mathsf{in},\textbf{a}}}
. This probability is at most
\delta_{\mathsf{in}}
by
9.4
, so

\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}\land\mathcal{E}_{1}]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{in}},

and going back to (
65
), we get

\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{in}}+\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}_{2}\;\land\;\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},2}].

(67)

It remains to bound the probability on the right hand side of (
67
). To this end, for each
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}],r\in[M]
let
\mathcal{E}_{j,r}
be the event that
T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}[\textbf{a}]
and
T_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(r)}[\textbf{b}]
satisfy the outer constraint,
\Phi_{\mathsf{out},(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})}
, or equivalently, the event that
(T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}[\textbf{a}])_{\to\textbf{b}}={(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j})}_{\to\textbf{b}}
. Also define the event
\mathcal{F}_{j}
to be the event that
D^{{\sf out}}_{t}(\textbf{a},T^{(j)}_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}[\textbf{a}])\notin\{\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{1}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t},\ldots,\left(y^{\textbf{a}}_{\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}}\right)_{\rightsquigarrow t}\}.

The probability on the right hand side of (
67
) can be bounded as

\begin{split}\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}_{2}\;\land\;\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{comp},2}]&\leqslant\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{r\in[M]}\left[\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}\left[\bigvee_{j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]}\mathcal{E}_{j,r}\land\mathcal{F}_{j}~\Bigg|~r\right]\right]\\
&\leqslant\sum_{j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in[M]\end{subarray}}\left[\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in[n]\\
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\sim\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{out}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{j,r}\land\mathcal{F}_{j}\right]\right].\end{split}

(68)

In the first transition, we use the observation that
r\in[M]
is chosen uniformly and independently of
t,\textbf{b},\textbf{a},b
in the distribution of the probability on the left. In addition, we use the fact that if
\mathcal{E}_{2}
occurs, it must be the case that for some
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
,
T^{(j)}_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}[\textbf{a}]
is consistent with
T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)]
on
b
and all
t
such that
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\in\supp(\mathcal{P}^{{\sf out}}_{t})
. Hence, if
\mathcal{E}_{2}\land\mathcal{E}_{{\sf comp},2}
occurs, then there must exist
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
such that

(T_{A_{\mathsf{out}}}^{(j)}[\textbf{a}])_{\to\textbf{b}}={(y^{\textbf{a}}_{j})}_{\to\textbf{b}}\quad\text{and}\quad D^{{\sf comp}}_{t}((\textbf{b},r),T_{A_{\mathsf{comp}}}[(\textbf{b},r)])=D^{{\sf out}}_{t}(\textbf{a},T^{(j)}_{{\sf out}}[\textbf{a}])\notin\left\{{\left(w^{(j^{\prime})}_{\ell}\right)}_{t}\right\}_{{j^{\prime}\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}],\ell\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{out}}]}}.

This takes care of the first transition. The second transition is by a union bound. Now fix a
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
and notice that the probability, in the summand of the last line is precisely the list-decoding error of
T^{(j)}_{A_{\mathsf{out}}},T^{(r)}_{B_{\mathsf{out}}}
, and hence for any
j\in[\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}]
and
r\in[M]
,

\Pr_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in[n]\\
(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})\sim\mathcal{P}^{\mathsf{out}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{j,r}\land\mathcal{F}_{j}\right]\leqslant\delta_{{\sf out}}.

Plugging this into (
68
) and going back to (
67
) we get that the list-decoding error is

\Pr_{(t,(\textbf{b},r),(\textbf{a};b))\sim\mathcal{Q}_{{\sf comp}}}[\mathcal{E}]\leqslant\delta_{\mathsf{in}}+\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{in}}\cdot\delta_{\mathsf{out}}.\qed

10
The Final dPCP

We are now ready to construct our final dPCP, using the constructions and tools developed up to this point. For the remainder of the section, fix a size
N
circuit
\varphi\colon\Sigma_{0}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
and let
\mathcal{L}:={\sf SAT}(\varphi)
.

10.1
An Intermediate dPCP with Nearly Constant Alphabet Size

Our starting point is the dPCP for
\mathcal{L}
given by
Theorem

8.4
which we denote by
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. By composing it with the inner dPCP from
Theorem

8.3
, we get the following result.

Theorem 10.1

(Intermediate dPCP)
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
there exist
L={\sf poly}(1/\varepsilon)
and

M=O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N))
,
d=O_{\varepsilon}(1)
,
k=O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log\log\log N))
such that the following holds. The language
\mathcal{L}
has a projection dPCP of the form

\mathcal{D}=\left(A\cup B,E,\Sigma_{A},\Sigma_{B},\{\Phi_{e}\}_{e\in E},\{D_{t}\}_{t\in[n]},\{\mathcal{P}_{t}\}_{t\in[n]}\right)

which satisfies the following

•

Length.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}\left(N\cdot{\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log N)\right)
.

•

Alphabet Sizes.
The left and right alphabet sizes are
2^{O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}\left({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log\log\log N)\right)}
and
O_{\varepsilon}(1)
respectively.

•

Decoding Degree.

O_{\varepsilon}(1)
.

•

Projection Decision Complexity.

O_{|\Sigma_{0}|}\left({\sf poly}_{\varepsilon}(\log\log\log N)\right)
.

•

Decoding Complexity.

O_{\varepsilon,|\Sigma_{0}|}(1)
.

•

Complete Decoding Distribution.
Call the complete decoding distribution
\mathcal{Q}
. Then the following holds:

–

For all
a\in A,b\in B
, we can write
\mathcal{Q}(\circ,a,b)=\frac{\mathsf{wt}(a,b)}{M}
for some integer
\mathsf{wt}(a,b)
.

–
